#### HLP-1 · MEDIUM · confirmed · bug
**Helper `UMask=0077` changes settings.json to mode 0600 on the first settings patch**  
`src/rdf_node/helper.py:93`

- **Masalah:** patch_file creates the replacement file with `os.open(..., st.st_mode&0o777)` and relies on that mode. rdf-control-helper.service sets `UMask=0077`, so the requested 0644 becomes 0600 and the file is then atomically renamed over settings.json. The owner is preserved by fchown, but group/other read is lost. Unless the _share directory carries the default ACL from `setup --grant-read` (ACL inheritance ignores the umask), the rdf-edge service and any other reader (for example the engine's web UI running as another user) can no longer read settings.json. The Edge then reports CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE, all DoA/Angular stop being LIVE, and later patches and config.get fail. The README presents the ACL grant as optional ('Setujui akses ACL...'), and on hosts where the home directory is 0755 an operator may reasonably decline it.
- **Dampak:** A single remote 'Apply safe settings' can permanently remove the Edge's read access to the engine settings, breaking LIVE telemetry and further control until someone fixes permissions by hand.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced (no setfacl in this container): a settings.json with mode 0644 was patched via helper.patch_file under os.umask(0o077). Output: `before: 0o644`, `after helper patch (UMask=0077, no default ACL): 0o600`.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Call `os.fchmod(out, st.st_mode & 0o7777)` after creating the temp file (fchmod is not affected by the umask), and copy the original file's ACL. Alternatively, relax UMask for this write path.
- **Verifikasi:** helper.py:93 creates the temp file through os.open with mode st.st_mode&0o777, so the umask applies. rdf-control-helper.service sets UMask=0077, the result is 0600, and os.replace installs it as settings.json. fchown keeps only the owner. With setup --grant-read, cli.py:50 sets a default ACL (d:u:rdf-edge:r-x) on the share dir; POSIX then ignores the umask and rdf-edge keeps read access, so the bug only appears when the Edge reads through world-readable bits without that ACL. The trigger is narrow (operator declined the ACL and the home dir is traversable), but when it fires the Edge loses read access to settings.json: CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE, no LIVE, and later patches fail.

#### MON-1 · MEDIUM · confirmed · bug
**Undervoltage and throttle monitoring cannot work under the installed rdf-edge account**  
`src/rdf_node/monitor.py:135`

- **Masalah:** Monitor reads throttling via `vcgencmd get_throttled`. On Raspberry Pi OS, /dev/vcio and /dev/vchiq are root:video 0660. install.sh creates `rdf-edge` as a system user without the video group, and rdf-edge.service sets no SupplementaryGroups. vcgencmd therefore fails, `throttled`/`undervoltage` stay null in telemetry/health/detail, and the panel's UNDERVOLTAGE error alert (agent.py:268) can never fire on a deployed node. It does work when run as a desktop user, so the gap is invisible in demo/dev.
- **Dampak:** Power-supply brownouts and thermal throttling on the field Raspberry are never alarmed on the panel or reported to Ground, although the panel advertises this alert.
- **Bukti:** Code trace: monitor.py:135-140 runs vcgencmd and silently ignores failures; install.sh:43 runs `useradd --system --user-group ... rdf-edge` with no extra groups; rdf-edge.service has no SupplementaryGroups=. The Pi udev rules assign vcio/vchiq to group video. Not run on a Pi.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Read `/sys/devices/platform/soc/soc:firmware/get_throttled` (world-readable on Pi kernels) or the hwmon undervoltage alarm instead, or add `SupplementaryGroups=video` to rdf-edge.service. Show 'tidak terbaca' explicitly when the value is null.
- **Verifikasi:** monitor.py:135-140 runs vcgencmd, and run() (monitor.py:10-14) returns None on a nonzero exit or error, so the values stay null. install.sh:44 creates rdf-edge with --user-group only, and rdf-edge.service sets no SupplementaryGroups. On Raspberry Pi OS, the udev rules make /dev/vcio and /dev/vchiq root:video 0660, so vcgencmd fails for that user ('VCHI initialization failed'). As a result, the UNDERVOLTAGE alert (agent.py:268) and the uv/throt telemetry can never fire. The panel does show 'unknown' (app.js:314) rather than a false OK, which softens the impact a little. Not verified on a Pi.

#### CLI-1 · MEDIUM · confirmed · bug
**`controls approve` applies lifecycle side effects before later prompts or the PPP preflight can abort, then saves nothing**  
`src/rdf_node/cli.py:152`

- **Masalah:** With `--lifecycle`, the command disables `sdr-watchdog.timer` (line 152) and writes /etc/systemd/system/<unit>.d/50-rdf-node-intent.conf (line 154) immediately. Only afterwards does it ask for REBOOT/SHUTDOWN, and run `require_ppp_service_ready()` and the 'T900 PPP RESTART' prompt. Any of these can `raise SystemExit` before helper.yaml and config.yaml are written (lines 171-172) and before daemon-reload or the service restarts. The README recommends exactly this combined command (`--lifecycle --ppp-restart --reboot --shutdown`, README.md:330), and the PPP preflight fails whenever t900-ppp.service is not active at that moment.
- **Dampak:** The operator ends up with the SDR watchdog disabled and a stop-guard drop-in installed, but lifecycle is not approved and other approvals are lost. SDR auto-recovery is gone without the Ground/Edge control that was meant to replace it.
- **Bukti:** Code trace of cli.py:142-174. The side effects at 148-154 come before the SystemExit paths at 158, 161, 165 and 167. Policy and config are persisted only at 171-172.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Collect and validate all confirmations and preflights first, then apply side effects and write the policy as one final step. Alternatively, persist each approved section before moving on to the next.
- **Verifikasi:** In cli.py:146-154, `systemctl disable --now sdr-watchdog.timer` and the 50-rdf-node-intent.conf drop-in are applied before the REBOOT/SHUTDOWN prompts (158,161), require_ppp_service_ready (163-164) and the 'T900 PPP RESTART' prompt (165-166), and any of these can raise SystemExit. helper.yaml and config.yaml are written only at 171-172. README.md:330 recommends exactly this combined command. Re-running recovers, because the watchdog is then already disabled and is not prompted again, but an operator who gives up is left with no watchdog and no lifecycle approval. Side note, unverified: require_ppp_service_ready insists on `Job=0` (helper.py:137-143), but real systemctl prints `Job=` (empty) …

#### RB-1 · MEDIUM · confirmed · robustness
**rollback.sh to a 1.0.0 release cannot start: the newer release rewrites config.yaml and helper.yaml with keys the old strict schema rejects**  
`scripts/rollback.sh:6`

- **Masalah:** Rollback only switches the `current` symlink and try-restarts services. On every Edge upgrade, install.sh runs `rdf-node setup` (install.sh:91), which always calls save_config and dumps the full current schema (mqtt.transport, mqtt.websocket_path, control.shutdown_enabled, control.ppp_restart_enabled, display.accent, display.font). `controls approve` likewise writes helper.yaml with allow_shutdown, allow_ppp_restart and allow_remote_control. The 1.0.0 load_config and helper policy loader reject unknown keys, so after rollback rdf-edge and rdf-control-helper fail to start. Neither the installer nor rollback.sh backs up these files. OPERATIONS.md 'Rollback code' presents rollback as the recovery path and warns only about databases and RF settings.
- **Dampak:** The documented recovery after a bad upgrade leaves the Raspberry bridge and helper down, at exactly the moment a working fallback is needed.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced: config.yaml written by the current save_config(load_config()) was loaded with the 1.0.0 sources (git archive 0aadd61) → `load_config FAILED: mqtt.transport: UNKNOWN_CONFIG_KEY`. Files: scratchpad/audit/current-written-config.yaml and old100/.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Have install.sh snapshot /etc/rdf-node/{config.yaml,helper.yaml} (and the Ground config) into the release directory before any rewrite, and have rollback.sh restore the snapshot that matches the previous release. At minimum, document that rollback across a schema change needs the old config restored.
- **Verifikasi:** Reproduced: a config written by the current save_config(load_config()) fails the 1.0.0 load_config with `mqtt.transport: UNKNOWN_CONFIG_KEY`. The current `controls approve` dumps the full policy including allow_shutdown, allow_ppp_restart and allow_remote_control (cli.py:170, helper.py:26-27). The 1.0.0 helper serve() rejects unknown policy keys with SystemExit. rollback.sh:6-8 only swaps the symlink and try-restarts. It prints that config was not reverted, but nothing warns that the old strict schema will reject it, and no backup of config.yaml or helper.yaml is kept. The documented recovery therefore fails.

#### PROV-1 · LOW · confirmed · robustness
**Provisioned broker certificate expires after 825 days and there is no renewal path**  
`scripts/provision-ground.sh:28`

- **Masalah:** The server certificate is issued with `-days 825`. provision-ground.sh refuses to run again once /etc/rdf-ground-mqtt/mosquitto.conf exists (line 13), and no script or doc describes re-issuing server.crt from the retained /etc/rdf-ground-mqtt/ca.key. The README troubleshooting for 'TLS error' only says to check CA/SAN/clock.
- **Dampak:** About 2 years 3 months after provisioning, Edge and Ground both fail with TLS_CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED and all telemetry and commands stop, with no documented recovery.
- **Bukti:** Code trace: provision-ground.sh:24 (CA 3650 days) and :28 (server 825 days); line 13 refuses a re-run; grep finds no renewal procedure in docs/README.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Add a `provision-ground.sh --renew-server-cert` mode (re-sign with the existing CA, keep credentials and ACL, reload Mosquitto) and document it, or issue the server certificate with the CA's lifetime. Optionally surface certificate expiry in Ground status.
- **Verifikasi:** provision-ground.sh:28 issues the server cert with -days 825. Line 13 refuses a re-run, and grep finds no renewal procedure in the docs or scripts. ca.key is kept at /etc/rdf-ground-mqtt/ca.key, so re-signing by hand is possible. The problem is real and causes a full outage when it hits. I downgraded it because the failure is more than two years out, manual recovery exists, and the validity period is partly a TLS-policy choice, which is near the excluded scope.

#### PROV-2 · LOW · confirmed · ux
**Provisioning aborts with an error when the PPP address is not up, skipping the bundle and import instructions, and cannot be re-run**  
`scripts/provision-ground.sh:118`

- **Masalah:** `systemctl enable --now rdf-ground-mqtt.service rdf-ground.service` waits for the start job. ExecStartPre runs wait-address.py for up to 60 s and exits 1 if 10.90.0.1 is absent, so the start job fails and `set -e` aborts the script. The final lines that print the bundle location and the `import-bundle` instruction never run. A re-run is refused because mosquitto.conf already exists. The README says the broker 'menunggu alamat PPP ... lalu retry', which implies provisioning works without the radio up.
- **Dampak:** Operators provisioning Ground before the T900 link is up see a failed provisioning, and get no bundle path or next-step instructions.
- **Bukti:** Code trace: provision-ground.sh:2 (`set -euo pipefail`), the generated unit's `ExecStartPre=... wait-address.py 10.90.0.1 60`, line 118 `enable --now`, lines 119-121 print the instructions; scripts/wait-address.py exits 1 after the timeout.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Use `systemctl enable rdf-ground-mqtt.service rdf-ground.service` followed by `systemctl start --no-block ...`, or tolerate a broker start failure with a warning, and always print the bundle/import instructions.
- **Verifikasi:** provision-ground.sh:118 `systemctl enable --now` waits for the start job, and the broker unit's ExecStartPre (wait-address.py) exits 1 after 60 s when 10.90.0.1 is absent. The mechanism is probably not an abort, though. With Restart=on-failure, systemd keeps the start job pending through auto-restarts, so the script most likely blocks indefinitely until the PPP address appears rather than exiting under set -e. In either case the bundle path and import-bundle instructions (lines 119-121) are not printed while the radio is down. That contradicts MQTT_GROUND.md:8, which says provisioning does not depend on the radio being up, and an operator who presses Ctrl-C cannot re-run the script (line …

#### DOC-2 · LOW · confirmed · doc-mismatch
**Docs promise anonymous MQTT when no credentials are set, but config validation rejects it**  
`src/rdf_node/config.py:102`

- **Masalah:** PROTOCOL.md:36, MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:35, MQTT_GROUND.md:66, IMPLEMENTATION.md:34 and CHANGELOG.md:94 say that without configured credentials the client uses an anonymous CONNECT. validate_config raises MQTT_CREDENTIALS_REQUIRED whenever `mqtt.enabled` is true and either credential file is null. Anonymous mode is therefore only reachable through the Edge panel's mqtt-ui-settings path. The Ground receiver, which has no panel, cannot be configured for an anonymous broker at all, and an Edge config.yaml written per the docs fails to load.
- **Dampak:** A deployment against an existing anonymous or plaintext broker as documented fails at service start, and the Ground cannot join such a broker at all.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced: a Ground config with mqtt.enabled=true, tls=false, allow_insecure_loopback=true and no credential files → `rejected: MQTT_CREDENTIALS_REQUIRED` (scratchpad/audit/anon-ground.yaml).
- **Saran perbaikan:** Either allow both credential files to be null when enabled (the Client already supports anonymous CONNECT) or state in the docs that file-based configs require credentials. Also note that Ground ignores `mqtt.enabled` and always connects.
- **Verifikasi:** config.py:100-102 raises MQTT_CREDENTIALS_REQUIRED when mqtt.enabled is true and either credential file is null. PROTOCOL.md:36, MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:35, MQTT_GROUND.md:66 and IMPLEMENTATION.md:34 all describe anonymous CONNECT without such a caveat, so an Edge config.yaml written per the docs fails to load. The claim that Ground cannot be anonymous 'at all' is overstated: Ground never reads mqtt.enabled (ground.py:34-39), so `enabled:false` with null credentials passes validation and connects anonymously. That workaround is non-obvious and undocumented.

#### CFG-2 · LOW · confirmed · robustness
**`rdf-node setup --share-dir/--engine-unit` after approval leaves helper.yaml pointing at the old targets**  
`src/rdf_node/cli.py:70`

- **Masalah:** `controls approve --settings/--lifecycle` copies `settings_path` and `engine_service` into /etc/rdf-node/helper.yaml. `setup` later changes only config.yaml (`source.share_dir` at line 68, `link.engine_service` at line 70) and does not update or warn about the helper policy. Afterwards the Edge reads the new folder or unit, while the helper patches the old settings.json, which always fails with CONFIG_CHANGED_BEFORE_WRITE or writes the wrong file. The helper also starts and stops the old unit while the monitor verifies the new one, which ends in OUTCOME_UNKNOWN.
- **Dampak:** Remote settings and lifecycle commands silently stop working after an operator re-runs the setup wizard, a step the README recommends.
- **Bukti:** Code trace: cli.py:66-74 versus cli.py:140 and 155, where the helper policy is written only in `controls approve`. helper.py uses policy['settings_path'] and policy['engine_service'] exclusively.
- **Saran perbaikan:** In setup, detect an existing approved helper policy whose path or unit differs, and either update it with confirmation or warn that `controls approve --settings/--lifecycle` must be re-run.
- **Verifikasi:** setup (cli.py:54-81) writes only config.yaml (source.share_dir, link.engine_service). The helper policy's settings_path and engine_service are written only by `controls approve` (cli.py:140,155,170). The helper uses only policy['settings_path'] and policy['engine_service'] (helper.py:271-297). After the share dir changes, the Edge computes expected_digest from the new file while the helper reads the old one, so every patch gets CONFIG_CHANGED_BEFORE_WRITE. Lifecycle commands target the old unit. setup prints no warning. This needs a re-run of setup after approval, so low.

#### KIO-1 · LOW · confirmed · bug
**`install.sh --ground --kiosk=USER` installs a kiosk that opens the Edge port 8790 instead of Ground's 8791**  
`deploy/kiosk/rdf-kiosk.service:11`

- **Masalah:** The installer accepts `--kiosk` with any role. The user unit hard-codes `Environment=RDF_NODE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8790`, but the Ground receiver listens on 8791 (install.sh sets api.port=8791 for Ground). The kiosk Chromium shows a connection error.
- **Dampak:** A Ground kiosk display shows an error page instead of the preview.
- **Bukti:** Code trace: install.sh:9-11 (no role check for --kiosk), install.sh:104-121 installs the same unit for both roles, and rdf-kiosk.service:11 hard-codes 8790.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Reject `--kiosk` for `--ground`, or write RDF_NODE_URL from the role or configured api.port when installing the user unit.
- **Verifikasi:** install.sh:11 accepts --kiosk with any role, and install.sh:102-127 installs the same user unit for both roles. deploy/kiosk/rdf-kiosk.service:11 hard-codes RDF_NODE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8790, while the Ground API is on 8791 (install.sh:54). Chromium on a Ground kiosk would get connection refused. This is a rare combination, so low.

#### KIO-2 · LOW · confirmed · robustness
**Kiosk install can leave ~/.config (and ~/.config/systemd) owned by root**  
`install.sh:108`

- **Masalah:** `install -d -o USER -g GROUP ~/.config/systemd/user ~/.config/autostart` applies the owner only to the last path component. Any missing parents (~/.config/systemd, and ~/.config itself on an account that has not logged into the desktop yet) are created as root:root 0755. The desktop user then cannot create files or directories there (for example compositor or app config under ~/.config).
- **Dampak:** The desktop session can break in subtle ways on a freshly created kiosk account.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced in scratch: `install -d -m 0755 -o nobody -g nogroup X/.config/systemd/user X/.config/autostart` → `.config` and `.config/systemd` are root:root, and only the leaf directories are nobody:nogroup.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Create each level explicitly with the user's ownership (`install -d -o ... ~/.config ~/.config/systemd ...`), or run the mkdir as the kiosk user (`runuser -u USER -- mkdir -p ...`).
- **Verifikasi:** Reproduced: `install -d -m 0755 -o nobody -g nogroup X/.config/systemd/user X/.config/autostart` leaves .config and .config/systemd as root:root 755; only the leaf directories are nobody:nogroup. install.sh:108 uses exactly this form. This only matters on an account whose ~/.config does not exist yet (never logged into the desktop), and then the desktop user cannot create other ~/.config entries. Low.

### Interop Mosquitto asli

_I ran a real Mosquitto 2.0.18 broker set up exactly as provision-ground.sh does: the CA and server cert from lines 22-28, the passwd/ACL from lines 39-53 hashed with mosquitto_passwd -U, and the config from lines 69-90 on 127.0.0.1:18803, run once each as TLS, plain TCP, ws:// and wss://. The one deviation was adding `user root`, because the mosquitto user cannot enter the scratch directory. In-process Edge Agents and Ground (fake share dir, fake helper socket, generated creds) worked end to end on every transport when run on Python 3.12. CONNACK properties {33:20, 34:10, 39:16384} passed mqtt.py:404-410. Every SUBACK was granted. Bulk published only telemetry/angular. The Will (r1,q1) was accepted and arrived at Ground within 1 s of kill -9. Mosquitto also publishes it on broker SIGTERM and persists it until the Edge reconnects. A Ground started late received retained state, capabilities and availability and went LIVE (detection valid, angular fresh). Retained data survived a broker restart and both sides recovered in about 3 s (Bulk resumed after the designed 20 s). Per-topic counts from Edge to broker and broker to Ground matched 1:1, so max_queued_messages 16, max_queued_bytes 32768 and queue_qos0_messages false did not drop anything (Edge never has more than one publish in flight). Round trips worked: stream.set, config.get to settings/reported over TLS, and ppp.restart through APPLIED on Ground. The libwebsockets handshake was accepted (101, 'Upgrade: WebSocket', single headers, subprotocol mqtt). New problems found only with the real broker: the provisioned CA breaks TLS on Python 3.13+, and Mosquitto hides ACL denials from READY and SENT. Already-known item with materially worse real-broker impact: on a shared client_id, Mosquitto publishes the kicked client's retained Will on every takeover, and Ground's node_state sid flips about once per second, wiping health and DoA. Ground then shows no fresh health most of the time. The docs (MQTT_GROUND.md, OPERATIONS.md, README.md) contain no mosquitto_sub/mosquitto_pub commands, and mosquitto-clients is not installed here. Scripted MQTT checks showed that viewer and controller creds both get SUBACK 0x01 for any filter (even cmd/#), and Mosquitto filters only at delivery. All scripts and logs are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-rdf-indicator-raspberry/67b9b04a-8e9b-512a-b5a0-e93d5cca1a8a/scratchpad/audit/mosq/._

#### MOSQ-1 · CRITICAL · confirmed · bug
**Provisioned CA has no keyUsage, so Python 3.13+ rejects the broker certificate and neither Edge nor Ground can connect over TLS**  
`rdf-node/scripts/provision-ground.sh:24`

- **Masalah:** Line 24 creates the CA with `openssl req -x509 -new ... -subj '/CN=RDF Local CA'` and no extensions. Ubuntu's openssl.cnf v3_ca section therefore gives the CA basicConstraints=critical,CA:TRUE plus SKID/AKID, but no keyUsage. Since Python 3.13, ssl.create_default_context() sets VERIFY_X509_STRICT (verify_flags 0x88020 here vs 0x8000 on 3.11/3.12). Under strict mode OpenSSL rejects any issuing CA without keyUsage. mqtt.py:290 uses exactly create_default_context(cafile=ca_file), so with the provisioned ca.crt every TLS handshake fails with 'CA cert does not include key usage extension'. The Edge Agent and Ground both end up in a permanent TLS_CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED reconnect loop: no telemetry, no state, no commands. This hits any host whose /usr/bin/python3 is 3.13 or newer: Raspberry Pi OS Trixie / Debian 13 (the current Pi default), Ubuntu 25.04+/26.04, and this container. README.md:61 and install.sh:19 accept Python 3.10+, and import-bundle (cli.py:93-94) and doctor never test the chain, so nothing warns the operator. selftest passes on 3.13 because tests/test_network.py builds a single self-signed cert, not the provisioner's CA-plus-leaf chain.
- **Dampak:** On current Raspberry Pi OS or Ubuntu releases, the documented install, provision and import-bundle path produces a system where MQTT never works on either side. The operator sees only MQTT ERROR, and there is no in-product way to fix it because the provisioner refuses to re-run.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced. I generated CA and server certs with provision-ground.sh lines 22-28. `openssl verify -x509_strict` fails with 'error 92 at 1 depth lookup: CA cert does not include key usage extension'. tlsprobe.py (rdf_node.mqtt.Client against the TLS Mosquitto listener) gives READY on python3.11 and python3.12, but on python3.13 it gives state ERROR, error TLS_CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED, with 'sslv3 alert certificate unknown' in the broker log. A full Edge Agent plus Ground on 3.13 (run/py313tls/edge.log, ground.log) both show TLS_CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED, generation 0, and 12 'certificate unknown' lines in mosq-tls-4.log. wss:// on 3.13 fails the same way. Fix check: re-issuing the CA with …
- **Saran perbaikan:** In provision-ground.sh:24 add `-addext 'keyUsage=critical,keyCertSign,cRLSign'` (and explicit basicConstraints=critical,CA:TRUE), then run `openssl verify -x509_strict -CAfile ca.crt server.crt` before installing. Provide a documented re-issue path for brokers already provisioned with the old CA. Add a regression test that builds the CA-plus-leaf chain with the provisioner's exact openssl commands and connects with the default context. Optionally make doctor/import-bundle do a strict test handshake. Do not clear VERIFY_X509_STRICT.
- **Verifikasi:** Reproduced independently: generated CA+leaf with the exact commands of scripts/provision-ground.sh:24-28 (CA gets only SKID/AKID + basicConstraints critical CA:TRUE, no keyUsage). `openssl verify -x509_strict` -> 'error 92 at 1 depth lookup: CA cert does not include key usage extension'. A client built like mqtt.py:290-292 (ssl.create_default_context(cafile=ca.crt), TLSv1.2 min) handshakes OK on python3.11 and 3.12 but FAILS on python3.13 with CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED 'CA cert does not include key usage extension'; server side sees 'sslv3 alert certificate unknown'. create_default_context verify_flags are 0x8000 on 3.11/3.12 vs 0x88020 on 3.13 (VERIFY_X509_STRICT). mqtt.py:261 maps this to …

#### MOSQ-2 · MEDIUM · confirmed · doc-mismatch
**On Mosquitto 2.x, ACL denials never become errors: SUBACK is always granted and denied QoS0 publishes show SENT, contrary to the docs**  
`rdf-node/docs/MQTT_GROUND.md:99`

- **Masalah:** MQTT_GROUND.md:99-100 says READY follows all SUBACKs and that an ACL rejection on subscribe/publish produces an error, not a silent green indicator. With the provisioned Mosquitto 2.0.18 acl_file, both claims are false. (1) Mosquitto returns SUBACK 0x01 for any subscription regardless of ACL and filters only at delivery, so mqtt.py:420 never sees a denial. If the control user lacks `topic read <prefix>/cmd/#` (a plausible mistake on the 'Memakai broker existing' path), the Edge Control client is READY, Ground's commands get PUBACK 0x00, and the Edge never receives them. Ground operations stay REQUESTED forever and a config.get then blocks 'Refresh config'. (2) An ACL-denied QoS0 publish is logged by Mosquitto as 'Denied PUBLISH' and silently dropped. mqtt.py:378 still sets _pub_result=0 and on_sent fires, so the Edge's mqtt_topic_delivery shows the topic as SENT (green TERKIRIM) although Ground never gets it. This applies to telemetry/health, doa, health/detail, diagnostic/doa and diagnostic/angular whenever a hand-written or older ACL misses a rule.
- **Dampak:** A broker ACL mistake is invisible: the Edge panel shows READY and green per-topic delivery and the Ground shows READY, while commands vanish or telemetry never arrives. Operators following the docs will trust the indicators and debug the wrong layer.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced. ACL without `topic read sdr/v2/uav-01/cmd/#` (acl-nocmdread, mosq-nocmdread.log): all 11 Edge cmd SUBACKs were sent, Edge was CONNECTED/True, and Ground's stream.set/config.get/ppp.restart each got 'Sending PUBACK to uav-01-ground (..., rc0)' with no delivery to uav-01-control. run/nocmdread/ground.log OPFINAL shows all three REQUESTED with result {}. aclprobe.py with uav-01-bulk creds: the QoS0 publish to telemetry/health gave 'Denied PUBLISH from aclprobe-bulk (d0, q0 ...)' in the broker log while the client called on_sent ('SENT(on_sent)') and stayed CONNECTED with no error. A raw SUBSCRIBE with viewer.json to sdr/v2/uav-01/cmd/# and # also returned SUBACK 0x01.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Correct MQTT_GROUND.md:99-100: SUBACK/READY and QoS0 send completion do not prove ACL permission on Mosquitto 2.x. Add an end-to-end check after provisioning or import, e.g. a scripted per-credential publish/subscribe self-test, or have Ground flag nodes whose commands get no ACK before expires_ms as 'no ACK, check broker ACL/route'. Consider showing QoS0 rows as 'SOCKET_WRITE (unconfirmed)' rather than plain SENT.
- **Verifikasi:** Reproduced on local Mosquitto 2.0.18 with rdf_node.mqtt.Client: a control user whose ACL lacks `topic read .../cmd/#` subscribed to cmd/# and got SUBACK granted (mqtt.py:420 sees no >1 code), Edge reported CONNECTED/ready=True; Ground's QoS1 publish to cmd/config/get got 'Sending PUBACK ... rc0' and the Edge on_message received nothing. A QoS0 publish by the Edge to a non-permitted topic logged 'Denied PUBLISH ... q0' while the client called on_sent (mqtt.py:378 sets _pub_result=0) and stayed CONNECTED. So MQTT_GROUND.md:99-100 ('ACL rejection on subscribe/publish produces an error, not a silent green indicator') is false on the only supported provisioning broker. The QoS0 half is partly …

#### MOSQ-3 · MEDIUM · confirmed · bug
**Ground ignores the broker's PUBACK 0x87 rejection of its own commands; following the documented write-path closure leaves every attempt REQUESTED forever**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/ground.py:370`

- **Masalah:** submit_command offers the command with `self.client.offer('cmd:'+id, ..., qos=1, expiry=15, priority=0)` and no on_error callback. When Mosquitto denies the publish, mqtt.py:344-347 delivers 'PUBLISH_REJECTED_0X87' to on_error, which is None, so the journal entry stays REQUESTED with result {} forever. OPERATIONS.md:95 tells operators to close the write path by revoking publish ACL on cmd/#. On Mosquitto every later click on a Ground action (including read-only config.get and operation.get, which also use cmd/#) behaves as follows. The broker sends PUBACK rc135. The Ground MQTT session is torn down and reconnects; this is the already-known PUBACK>=0x80 behaviour. The operation shows as pending with no reason. For config.get, settings_request_id stays set, so 'Refresh config' then returns SETTINGS_REQUEST_PENDING. The same path also drops other asynchronous send failures, such as an outbox cleared by a disconnect before the command left Ground.
- **Dampak:** After an operator deliberately blocks remote writes, Ground keeps offering the buttons, records phantom in-progress commands that never resolve, and interrupts its own telemetry session on each click, with no visible 'not authorized' feedback.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with an ACL without the controller's `topic write sdr/v2/uav-01/cmd/#` (acl-nocmdwrite, plain listener, mosq-nocmdwrite.log). For each of stream.set, config.get and ppp.restart the broker logged 'Denied PUBLISH from uav-01-ground ...', then 'Sending PUBACK to uav-01-ground (mNN, rc135)', then 'Client uav-01-ground closed its connection', then a reconnect (Ground reconnects 0 -> 1 -> 2). run/nocmdwrite/ground.log OPFINAL shows all three operations in stage REQUESTED with result {}.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Pass on_error to client.offer in submit_command. On PUBLISH_REJECTED_0x87, OUTBOX_* or MQTT_DISCONNECTED before transmission, update the journal to REJECTED with a readable reason (e.g. BROKER_NOT_AUTHORIZED / NOT_SENT) and clear settings_request_id for config.get. If the connection reset after the bytes were written, use OUTCOME_UNKNOWN. Note in OPERATIONS.md:95 that revoking cmd/# also disables config.get/operation.get.
- **Verifikasi:** ground.py:370-371 calls self.client.offer(... qos=1, expiry=15, priority=0) with no on_error. mqtt.py:344-347 delivers PUBLISH_REJECTED_0X87 only through m.on_error (_notify_error at mqtt.py:140 is a no-op for None) and then raises PUBLISH_REJECTED, tearing down the session (I observed this reconnect on an Edge client in the same Mosquitto probe: on_error got PUBLISH_REJECTED_0X87, reconnects=1). Nothing else transitions the journal: REQUESTED is set at ground.py:369, and there is no expires_ms sweep in ground.py or web/ground.js (grep shows expires_ms only at ground.py:367). settings_request_id (set at :374) is only cleared at :132 (Edge sid change), :152 (settings report), :242 (terminal …

#### MOSQ-4 · LOW · refuted · ux
**PUBACK 0x10 'No matching subscribers' is recorded as delivered for non-retained ACKs and settings reports**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/mqtt.py:348`

- **Masalah:** Mosquitto returns PUBACK reason 0x10 for a QoS1 publish with no matching subscription. Because Ground uses Session Expiry 0, this happens whenever Ground is disconnected, restarting or not yet subscribed. mqtt.py:344-349 treats any reason below 0x80 as success, so agent.py:334 records ack/operation, ack/config and settings/reported as SENT with confirmation PUBACK. For these non-retained topics, 0x10 means the message was discarded and will never reach Ground: the operation result is lost and a settings report leaves Ground's request pending. The Edge already has the broker telling it so but discards the information, and the panel shows green delivery.
- **Dampak:** The Edge panel claims an ACK or settings report was delivered when the broker reported that no Ground was listening. This hides the root cause of Ground operations that later appear stuck.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced on the plain listener (mosq-plain-2.log:563-564). A Ground-credential client published cmd/operation/get and disconnected immediately. The Edge then published ack/operation, the broker logged 'Sending PUBACK to uav-01-control (m29, rc16)', and the Edge snapshot reported mqtt_topic_delivery['ack/operation'] = {'state':'SENT','qos':1,'confirmation':'PUBACK','error':None}. In the TLS run every QoS1 publish made before Ground connected also got rc16.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Pass the PUBACK reason to on_sent (or a separate callback). For non-retained topics (ack/*, settings/reported), record a distinct state such as NO_SUBSCRIBER instead of SENT, and optionally keep a settings report pending for a short retry window.
- **Verifikasi:** Mechanics are real: on Mosquitto 2.0.18 a QoS1 publish to a topic with no subscriber got 'Sending PUBACK ... rc16' and mqtt.py:344-349 treated it as success, so agent.py:333 records state SENT, confirmation PUBACK. But the claimed observable wrong result ('panel claims an ACK or settings report was delivered' to Ground) does not happen: the panel labels it 'TERKIRIM' with 'PUBACK broker' (web/app.js:122-123) and the caption at web/app.js:82 explicitly says QoS 1 means broker PUBACK, not Ground processing confirmation; MQTT_GROUND.md:105-107 says the same. SENT = broker accepted is accurate per the documented contract. Surfacing rc 0x10 as NO_SUBSCRIBER would be a diagnostic enhancement, not …

### Kontrak waktu Edge–Ground

_Edge and Ground compare raw wall-clock timestamps across hosts. Ground checks every Edge `t` and source timestamp against its own clock and allows at most 1 s in the future (ground.py:61). The Edge checks Ground's `issued_ms` against its clock with the same 1 s allowance (control.py:55). Everything works only while the Edge-Ground clock offset S stays within about 1 s plus the one-way delay L. Outside that window, Ground silently discards all telemetry, or the Edge rejects every command. Neither panel ever says the cause is clock skew. The package provides no time source in the documented PPP-only field topology and never checks Ground's own clock. As a result, `clock_trusted`, and with it LIVE DoA and every Edge mutation, cannot be reached in the field. An Edge without an RTC that reboots stays permanently invisible to Ground. All thresholds below were reproduced with scripts in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-rdf-indicator-raspberry/67b9b04a-8e9b-512a-b5a0-e93d5cca1a8a/scratchpad/audit/._

#### TIME-1 · MEDIUM · confirmed · data-correctness
**Ground silently drops all health, DoA, angular and settings reports when the Edge clock is ~1 s (+link delay) ahead, or 8 s minus delay behind**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/ground.py:61`

- **Masalah:** _fresh() requires -1000 <= ground_now - t <= limit. Ground sees age = A + L - S, where A is the source age at send, L the one-way delay and S = Edge - Ground.
- telemetry/health: t = Edge now_ms (agent.py:370), limit 8000. Rejected when S > L+1000 ms or S < L-8000 ms.
- telemetry/doa: t = source timestamp, limit 5000. Rejected when S > A+L+1000 or A+L-S > 5000.
- telemetry/angular: limit 10000. settings/reported: limit 30000.
The ValueError is swallowed in _loop (ground.py:54); only the hidden link.rejected counter moves.
Downstream effects:
- DoA and angular are then also rejected as *_WITHOUT_HEALTH.
- submit_command returns NODE_NOT_FRESH for every mutation.
- A rejected settings/reported leaves settings_request_id set, so the next 'Refresh config' returns SETTINGS_REQUEST_PENDING permanently. Skew is a deterministic trigger of that known wedge.
What the operator sees:
- Ground preview: state pill 'LIVE / HEALTH STALE / MQTT CONNECTED' and Health tile 'BELUM ADA' or 'STALE'. 'Jam node' shows '--' because ground.js:584 reads only health.clk, even though node_state.clock is present. All buttons show 'Perintah memerlukan health node yang fresh.' Diagnostic DoA, which has no time check, keeps arriving as 'RECEIVED', so the link looks fine.
- Edge panel (no receipts by design): MQTT CONNECTED, every topic TERKIRIM, detection valid, no alert.
Nothing on either side mentions clock skew. The code that docs/MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:617 tells operators to check (SOURCE_TIMESTAMP_NOT_FRESH) is never exposed.
- **Dampak:** A Ground clock that lags a synced Edge by about 1.3 s makes Ground lose all monitoring and control. Typical laptop RTC drift after a day without NTP is enough. The operator sees a connected broker and arriving diagnostics, so the cause is not discoverable from either UI.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with skew_unit.py (Ground acceptance by age, exact boundaries):
- health: OK for -1000..8000 ms, rejected at -1001 and 8001.
- DoA: rejected above 5000.
- angular: rejected at -1001 and 10001.
Reproduced end to end with e2e_skew.py (real Agent + Ground over tests/broker_fixture, now_ms patched per module, delivery delayed by L); results in sweep.jsonl.
- L=300, S=+1500 / +5000 / +10000 / +20000: health_fresh False, detection False, link_rejected 10-24 (all SOURCE_TIMESTAMP_NOT_FRESH), Ground submit NODE_NOT_FRESH. The Edge snapshot at the same moment shows mqtt CONNECTED, all topics SENT, detection valid, no alerts.
- L=3000: the limit moves to S > 4 s; S=+5000 failed the same way.
- Edge behind: S=-10000 at L=300 and S=-5000 at L=3000 also lost all health.
- Settings: a report with t=now+1500 was rejected with SOURCE_TIMESTAMP_NOT_FRESH, then config.get returned REJECTED SETTINGS_REQUEST_PENDING.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Make Ground freshness independent of cross-host clocks. Use Ground monotonic receipt time plus an age computed on the Edge, bounded by the MQTT/outbox expiry. Health already carries `age`; add a source-age field to doa and angular. Alternatively, estimate the offset from state/health `t` against receipt time and apply the tolerance to the corrected value. Keep the last rejection reason per topic and show 'CLOCK SKEW ±X s' in the preview. Show node_state.clock when health is missing.
- **Verifikasi:** I reproduced it with my own script (verify_time/v1.py). Ground._fresh (ground.py:59-61) accepts health only when -1000 <= ground_now - t <= 8000. Health with t=now+1001 or t=now-8100 raises SOURCE_TIMESTAMP_NOT_FRESH. _loop (ground.py:54) swallows the reason and only increments link.rejected, which ground.js never renders. After that, health_fresh is False and submit_command returns NODE_NOT_FRESH. A config.get whose settings/reported carries t=now+1500 is rejected, and the next config.get returns SETTINGS_REQUEST_PENDING. That flag is cleared only by a new sid or a non-APPLIED terminal ack (ground.py:243-245), and the Edge acks config.get as APPLIED. ground.js:584 reads only health.clk, so …

#### TIME-2 · MEDIUM · confirmed · bug
**Edge rejects every Ground command as EXPIRED_OR_INVALID_DEADLINE when its clock is ~1 s (+delay) behind Ground, while Ground shows the node healthy**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/control.py:55`

- **Masalah:** Ground stamps issued_ms and expires_ms = issued+15000 with its own clock (ground.py:367). The Edge raises EXPIRED_OR_INVALID_DEADLINE when issued > edge_now+1000, i.e. S < -(L+1000), or when expires <= edge_now, i.e. S >= 15000-L.
Ground keeps accepting health until S < L-8000. So for S in [L-8000, -(L+1000)) Ground shows HEALTH FRESH, 'Jam node SINKRON' and every button enabled, and every click goes REQUESTED -> REJECTED. This covers stream.set, config.patch, processing, restart, PPP, reboot, shutdown, and also config.get/operation.get.
The same Edge lag also eats the 5 s DoA window (A+L-S > 5000), so the Ground angle flickers and then shows '--' with no reason.
The error code merges 'expired' with 'issued in the future', so the operator investigates link latency, not clocks. The Edge panel shows only the op as REJECTED.
Plausible trigger: Ground's clock is stepped by NTP by more than 1 s while the Edge's timesyncd polls only every up to 2048 s.
- **Dampak:** Remote control (lifecycle, settings, PPP restart, reboot, shutdown) stops working whenever Ground's clock is ahead of the Edge by about 1.3 s, while the Ground UI says the node is fresh and ready. The error text points the operator at latency, not clocks.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with skew_unit.py (submit at Edge time = Ground + S + L):
- REJECTED for S=-1500 at L=300, accepted at L=1000 and 3000.
- REJECTED for S <= -5000 at every L.
- REJECTED for S=+14000 at L >= 1000, and for S >= 15000.
End to end (sweep.jsonl):
- L=300, S=-1500: Ground health_fresh True, detection valid, submit REQUESTED, final REJECTED {error: EXPIRED_OR_INVALID_DEADLINE}.
- L=300, S=-5000: health fresh, 7 DoA rejected, command REJECTED.
- e2e_validfrac.py at L=300: Ground detection.valid fraction 1.0 at -2.5 s, 0.96 at -3 s, 0.84 at -3.5 s, 0.23 at -4 s.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Return distinct codes: COMMAND_EXPIRED versus COMMAND_ISSUED_IN_FUTURE / CLOCK_SKEW, with edge_now_ms in the result. Validate the TTL relative to Edge receipt (expires-issued <= 30 s, plus a receipt-time window) instead of comparing absolute clocks with a 1 s allowance. Let Ground warn when its estimated offset to the node exceeds the tolerance.
- **Verifikasi:** control.py:54-55 rejects when issued > edge_now+1000 or expires <= edge_now, and Ground stamps issued_ms/expires_ms with its own clock (ground.py:367). Reproduced: S=-1100 and S=-1500 return EXPIRED_OR_INVALID_DEADLINE, S=-900 passes. S=+15000 is rejected. The check runs before the READ_OPS branch, so config.get and operation.get are rejected too. For an Edge lag between ~L+1 s and 8 s-L, Ground still accepts health (ground.py:157), so the preview shows HEALTH FRESH, 'Jam node SINKRON' and enabled buttons while every command is rejected. 'Expired' and 'issued in future' share one error code. Same root cause and trigger class as TIME-1 (Ground clock unsynced while the Edge is synced). The …

#### TIME-3 · LOW · confirmed · doc-mismatch
**The documented field deployment has no time source, so clock_trusted, LIVE/DoA telemetry and every Edge mutation are unreachable in the field; Ground's own clock is never checked**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/monitor.py:68`

- **Masalah:** Why clock_trusted cannot be reached in the field:
- clock_trusted is true only when timedatectl NTPSynchronized is 'yes' (monitor.py:68-69).
- In the field the Edge can reach only 10.90.0.1 over PPP/T900.
- install.sh and scripts/provision-ground.sh configure no NTP server on Ground and no NTP target on the Edge.
- README.md:264-266 says the package does not create NTP over T900. IMPLEMENTATION.md:132-133 says unknown time closes the write/live gates.
- No document says Ground must serve time or that the two clocks must agree within about 1 s.
- NTPSynchronized is 'no' on every boot until an NTP client syncs. The kernel unsync flag also returns roughly 9 h after the last update (maxerror reaches 16 s at 500 ppm).
Result: after any boot in the field, clock_trusted stays False permanently.
Consequences:
- 'CLOCK_UNTRUSTED' in detection.reasons makes unverified_only False (agent.py:366-367), so telemetry/doa is never published, neither ok=1 nor ok=0.
- Bulk angular pauses with SOURCE_NOT_ELIGIBLE.
- Every Ground and panel mutation is rejected with CLOCK_UNTRUSTED (control.py:85).
- The Ground preview keeps those buttons enabled: mutationBlockReason (ground.js:16-24) ignores health.clk, although 'Jam node TIDAK DIPERCAYA' is shown.
Ground's own clock: ground.py never probes it, the snapshot does not expose it, and the preview does not display it. Yet it is the reference for every freshness check and command deadline (TIME-1/TIME-2).
- **Dampak:** In the field, any reboot or power cycle permanently disables the primary DoA feed to Ground and all remote and local control, unless the operator independently builds an NTP path over PPP that no document describes. A drifting Ground clock is never detected.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with e2e_untrusted.py (S=0, L=300, Edge monitor clock_trusted=False):
- The broker saw only telemetry/health x13, health/detail, state, capabilities and one diagnostic/angular. There was no telemetry/doa and no telemetry/angular.
- bulk_reason was SOURCE_NOT_ELIGIBLE and Ground showed health_clk=0, detection False.
- Ground stream.set was REQUESTED, then REJECTED {error: CLOCK_UNTRUSTED}.
The absence of a time source was confirmed by grepping install.sh, provision-ground.sh and deploy/systemd: no ntp, chrony or timesync entries.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Make time sync part of provisioning:
- In provision-ground.sh, configure chrony on Ground to serve 10.90.0.1 (allow 10.90.0.0/30, local stratum).
- In install/import-bundle, set NTP=10.90.0.1 for timesyncd on the Edge.
- Document the ±1 s requirement in README and OPERATIONS.
- Add a Ground clock-trust probe and show it in the preview.
- Gate Ground buttons on health.clk, with a clear 'Jam node tidak dipercaya' reason.
- **Verifikasi:** The headline is overstated. README.md:264-266 says time sync must be fixed through OS management/NTP and that the package deliberately does not create NTP over T900. MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:617 tells operators to check both hosts' clocks. IMPLEMENTATION.md:132 documents that unknown time closes the live and write gates. So clock_trusted gating DoA, Bulk and mutations (control.py:85, agent.py:366-367) is intended behavior, not a doc mismatch, and clock_trusted is reachable when the operator sets up NTP as instructed. What survives: (1) mutationBlockReason (ground.js:16-24) ignores health.clk. With clk=0, Ground shows 'Jam node TIDAK DIPERCAYA' but leaves the buttons enabled, and each click …

#### TIME-4 · LOW · confirmed · robustness
**After an Edge reboot or power cycle without a powered RTC, Ground never accepts health again: reboot never confirms, node looks dead, no command can be sent**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/ground.py:157`

- **Masalah:** fake-hwclock and the timesyncd clock file restore the time saved at shutdown, or at the last hourly save after a power loss. This applies to a Pi 4, or a Pi 5 without the RTC battery after a power cycle. The Edge then runs behind by at least the downtime: 20-60 s for a reboot, up to about 1 h after a battery swap. Without NTP (TIME-3) it never catches up.
The Edge is self-consistent, because the SDR files use the same clock, so it only shows CLOCK_UNTRUSTED. On Ground:
- Every health message whose lag exceeds 8000-L is rejected (ground.py:157).
- REBOOT_SCHEDULED never reaches APPLIED, because confirmation needs an accepted health (ground.py:161-163).
- health_fresh stays False and submit_command returns NODE_NOT_FRESH for everything, including shutdown and PPP restart (ground.py:325).
- The retained state with clock UNTRUSTED is accepted, but the preview shows 'Jam node --'.
- telemetry/diagnostic/doa has no time check (ground.py:195-211). It is shown as 'UNVERIFIED / RECEIVED' (stale=false) with a 'TIME sumber' minutes behind Ground and no warning. So the only live-looking data is diagnostic.
A forward NTP step, when one happens, fixes Ground within about 1 s.
- **Dampak:** One routine power cycle in the field (battery swap, or a commanded reboot on hardware without an RTC) leaves Ground unable to monitor or control the node for the rest of the mission. The UI gives no hint that the clock is the cause.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with reboot_fakehwclock2.py (new boot and sid; health sent with the Edge D s behind):
- D=5: reboot APPLIED.
- D=9 / 30 / 60: health SOURCE_TIMESTAMP_NOT_FRESH, health_fresh False, health.clk None, node_state.clock UNTRUSTED, reboot stage stays REBOOT_SCHEDULED, stream.set REJECTED NODE_NOT_FRESH. Diagnostic DoA accepted with stale=False and source lag 9.2 / 30.2 / 60.2 s.
Reproduced with e2e_step.py (Edge 600 s behind, then forward step):
- For 10 s, link.rejected grew every second and health was never accepted.
- Diagnostic DoA was shown with a source lag of about 601-603 s and stale=False.
- After the step, Ground health was fresh within about 1 s.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Apply the skew-independent freshness from TIME-1. Treat a large constant offset on a new boot as a reported CLOCK_OFFSET condition, not a reason to drop health. Confirm reboot by the new boot id plus Ground-side receipt freshness. Flag diagnostic DoA whose source time disagrees with Ground time beyond tolerance. Ship time sync as in TIME-3.
- **Verifikasi:** The mechanism is real. Once the new state is accepted, health with t more than 8000-L behind is rejected (ground.py:157). REBOOT_SCHEDULED reaches APPLIED only on accepted health (ground.py:161-163), and submit_command returns NODE_NOT_FRESH (ground.py:325). But this is the Edge-behind case of TIME-1, not a separate root cause. With NTP present, as README.md:264-266 requires, it lasts only until timesyncd syncs after boot, then health and reboot confirmation recover. It is persistent only in a no-NTP deployment. There the Edge already rejects every mutation with CLOCK_UNTRUSTED (control.py:85), and fake-hwclock would make the clock wrong from the very first field boot, not just after a …

#### TIME-5 · LOW · confirmed · data-correctness
**PPP restart marked APPLIED from a health message the Edge sent before it even received the restart command**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/ground.py:71`

- **Masalah:** _reconcile_ppp_restart_health compares the Edge health `t` (Edge clock) with the request's issued_ms (Ground clock). Any health sent after Ground's issue time passes, including health sent while the command was still in transit.
The Edge publishes the PPP_RESTART_REQUESTED ACK as soon as `systemctl --no-block restart` returns, usually before pppd tears the link down. Ground therefore usually marks the record APPLIED ('SYSTEMD_ACCEPTED_AND_FRESH_NODE_HEALTH'; UI: 'Ground menerima health node fresh yang lebih baru') before the restart has taken effect. An Edge clock ahead of Ground widens the window.
APPLIED is terminal, so the record leaves journal.pending_ppp_restarts(). The confirm_previous_unknown / PPP_RESTART_HEALTH_NOT_NEWER safeguard (ground.py:341-353) then does not apply to an immediate second restart while the link is still cycling. The browser gate in pppReadiness (ground.js:141-147) has the same cross-clock comparison.
- **Dampak:** Ground reports a PPP restart as APPLIED, with fresh health after the request, before the link has gone down. This misleads the operator and disables the unresolved-restart confirmation meant to stop repeated restarts.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with e2e_skew.py in ppp mode (Edge helper mocked to {requested:true}); results in ppp.jsonl. Final stage was APPLIED in every run. Proof health timestamp minus Edge command-receipt time:
- S=0, L=300: -114 ms
- S=0, L=1000: -808 ms
- S=0, L=2000: -795 ms
- S=+800, L=300: -126 ms
- S=-1000, L=300: +888 ms (the only run where the proof health came after the command arrived)
- **Saran perbaikan:** Compare timestamps from one clock only. Require a health `t` greater than the Edge-side ACK `t` or a requested-at time stamped by the Edge. Preferably also require evidence that the link actually cycled and returned: a new MQTT generation or a receipt gap seen on Ground's monotonic clock. Apply the same rule in the browser gate.
- **Verifikasi:** _reconcile_ppp_restart_health (ground.py:62-76) compares the Edge health t with requested_ms == the request's Ground issued_ms. With synced clocks, a health the Edge sent between Ground's issue time and its own receipt of the command passes 'health_ms > requested_ms'. Reconcile also runs as soon as the PPP_RESTART_REQUESTED ack arrives (ground.py:246-247), so APPLIED can be set before pppd has gone down. ground.js:141-147 has the same comparison. Severity is low, not medium. CONTROL.md:109-111 and the UI text (ground.js:107) state that APPLIED is not proof of PPP or link recovery, so the operator is not told the link recovered. An immediate second restart is also blocked by the helper …

#### TIME-7 · LOW · confirmed · bug
**Edge 'last operation' ordering uses wall-clock time, so after a no-RTC power loss new operations sort below the previous boot's**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/journal.py:57`

- **Masalah:** latest() orders by `updated` (wall-clock now_ms written by accept/update, journal.py:48/53). After a power loss, fake-hwclock restores the last hourly save, up to about 1 h earlier than the previous boot's last activity.
Operations recorded in the new boot get smaller `updated` values than the previous boot's last operations. These include Ground commands rejected with CLOCK_UNTRUSTED, config.get reads, and recover() markings. The Edge panel's 'Operasi terakhir' and its toast (agent.py:264/286) then show the previous boot's operation, and prune keeps the wrong rows.
- **Dampak:** Right after a field power cycle, the Edge panel shows a stale operation result as the latest one.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with reboot_fakehwclock2.py: an op written at T, then a new op written with the clock at T-40 min+5 s. journal.latest(1) returned 'prev-boot-op' instead of 'new-boot-op'.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Order by a monotonic insertion sequence (rowid/AUTOINCREMENT, or boot plus sequence), not by wall-clock updated_ms.
- **Verifikasi:** Journal.latest (journal.py:55-57) orders by wall-clock `updated`. If the clock moves backward after an unclean power loss, newer rows sort below older ones; that part is real. The cited trigger is wrong, though. Commands rejected with CLOCK_UNTRUSTED (and every other pre-accept rejection) are never journaled: control.py:85 raises before journal.accept, and the except path only sends an ACK. Mutations are accepted only when clock_trusted, so they carry correct post-sync times. The only realistic rows that mis-sort are Ground read ops (config.get/operation.get journaled at control.py:73-78) and recover() markings written before NTP sync after a power loss. The effect is that 'Operasi …

#### TIME-6 · LOW · refuted · data-correctness
**/api/v2/angular/diagnostic/latest labels frames SOURCE_STALE most of the time even with perfect clocks**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/ground.py:298`

- **Masalah:** diagnostic_angular_view compares Ground now_ms with the Edge source timestamp at view time, against cfg freshness.doa_ms (2500 ms, the Edge's local source-freshness value).
In the default commissioning state (authority and angle unverified), regular Bulk frames go to this view and arrive about every 9 s. Diagnostic frames arrive every 6 or 30 s. So the frame is marked SOURCE_STALE for most of each interval even with zero skew, and immediately on arrival once the Edge lags by about 2 s.
The preview does not show this endpoint (known), but MQTT_GROUND.md:125 tells operators and external dashboards to read it.
- **Dampak:** External Ground dashboards using the documented diagnostic endpoint see the only available curve flagged stale about 3/4 of the time, and always once the clocks drift.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with e2e_diag_angular.py (default unverified config, real Bulk budget 350 B/s):
- S=0, L=300: 66 of 86 samples (77%) stale; frames at q 6, 41, 77, 113, 148; the first view was already 751 ms old.
- S=-1500: 51 of 53 (96%) stale; arrival age 2471 ms.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Judge staleness by receipt age (monotonic) against the expected cadence plus link budget, using the source age reported by the Edge. Do not reuse the Edge's 2.5 s local freshness value as a cross-host threshold.
- **Verifikasi:** In the auditor's scenario (default config, authority and angle unverified), diagnostic_angular_view always adds SOURCE_AUTHORITY_UNVERIFIED_AT_EDGE and ANGLE_UNVERIFIED_AT_EDGE (ground.py:285-287), so `stale` is True 100% of the time whatever the age. My repro (verify_time/v2.py) gives a 700 ms frame stale=True. SOURCE_STALE only adds a reason code and does not change the observable flag. The 77% figure counts the reason, not the flag. When flags are complete (frames sent on diagnostic/angular while Bulk is blocked), a frame older than freshness.doa_ms (2500) labeled SOURCE_STALE is accurate. The docs say timestamp age feeds stale/validation_reasons (PROTOCOL.md:203-209), and the endpoint …

### Kesesuaian engine KrakenSDR & retune

_I compared the Edge source parsers and the config.patch flow with the real upstream writers (krakenrf/krakensdr_doa @2e1c4e6, cloned to scratchpad/audit/upstream). I then drove the real Agent, helper, Ground and Edge API against an engine simulator built from upstream behaviour: a 0.5 s ext_upd_flag watcher, int() conversions, Auto-squelch write-back, indent=2 saves and upstream-shaped status/CSV/XML (scratchpad/audit/sim). Health parsing matches upstream: status.json has timestamp_ms, daq_ok, daq_num_dropped_frames and daq_status.frame_sync/sample_delay_sync/iq_sync/data_frame_index, so DAQ HEALTHY and LIVE are reachable. The 377-field ", "-separated CSV and the doa.xml DATA/TIME/DOA/FREQUENCY tags parse. Upstream never writes rf_center_freq or gain_db, though, and several normal upstream settings shapes (Auto gain, Auto squelch, float VFO, output_vfo ALL) break the Edge. Other measured answers: CONFIG_CHANGED_BEFORE_WRITE cannot happen through the UIs (the worker stays BUSY for 12 s, and the engine write-back lands in the same 0.5 s watcher tick). It does fail a patch sent within about 0.35 s of another settings.json write (2 of 6 runs). Re-formatting center_freq does not change the revision except on the truncation channels (finding NEC-6). A follow-up Ground command sent right after the ACK got no CONFIG_REVISION_CONFLICT._

#### NEC-1 · HIGH · confirmed · data-correctness
**KrakenSDR Auto gain (uniform_gain:"Auto") makes the whole settings file unreadable: no DoA, no config report, and no remote way to fix it**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/source.py:25`

- **Masalah:** Upstream save_configuration writes uniform_gain = "Auto" whenever AGC is selected (kraken_web_interface.py:278-280). AGC is a normal option in the KrakenSDR DAQ card (daq_config_card.py:88), and the watcher handles the string explicitly (utils.py:264-268). safe_settings() runs finite(data['uniform_gain']) for every FIELD_MAP key, so float('Auto') raises ValueError. Source.poll then sets config_error='ValueError:SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE' and never sets settings, safe_digest, revision or raw_digest (source.py:139-151). Effects: (1) view() always adds CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE and CONFIG_ATTRIBUTION_UNVERIFIED, so detection is never VALID. Because those reasons are not 'unverified only', no telemetry/doa is sent at all (not even ok=0) and Bulk angular is permanently SOURCE_NOT_ELIGIBLE. (2) config.get is rejected with SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE (control.py:63-64). (3) config.patch, including the gain_db patch that would fix it, is ACCEPTED (base_rev None == revision None) and then FAILED SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE in the worker (control.py:164-165). The Ground form stays disabled ('Revision config node belum dilaporkan'). The panel only shows a CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE warning and gives no hint that AGC is the cause. The plan requires AGC mode to be shown as a distinct state ('AGC mode dibedakan', Rancangan line 914). No doc says AGC is unsupported.
- **Dampak:** If the engine runs in AGC mode, the node sends no DoA of any kind to Ground. Operators cannot read the settings remotely or switch to manual gain, and the only visible symptom is a generic CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced end to end with the real Agent, helper socket, Ground and in-process bus (scratchpad/audit/sim/run_scenarios.py F, engine started with uniform_gain 'Auto'). Output: valid=False reasons=['CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE','CONFIG_ATTRIBUTION_UNVERIFIED']; sdr_revision=None safe={} config_error=ValueError:SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE; Ground config.get -> Ground journal REJECTED {'error':'SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE'}; Ground config.patch gain 20.7 -> FAILED {'error':'SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE'}; telemetry/angular published: 0, telemetry/doa ok=1 published: 0. Parser-level check: safe_settings(...uniform_gain='Auto') -> ValueError could not convert string to float: 'Auto'.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Parse uniform_gain == 'Auto' as an explicit state (e.g. gain_mode:'AUTO', gain_db:null) instead of raising. Keep revision, raw_digest and attribution working, and show 'AGC' in both panels. Allow a gain_db patch to replace 'Auto' with a manual value through the helper, which should also write the matching mode. Add a regression test with upstream-shaped settings that contain uniform_gain:'Auto'.
- **Verifikasi:** Confirmed in the upstream clone: kraken_web_interface.py:278-280 writes uniform_gain="Auto" for AGC, which is a normal dropdown option (daq_config_card.py:88), and the watcher handles it at utils.py:264-268. In the node, safe_settings (source.py:25) calls finite('Auto') -> ValueError. Poll raises before revision, safe_digest and raw_digest are set (source.py:141-151). My Source-level repro gave config_error=ValueError:SETTINGS_UNAVAILABLE, revision=None, valid=False, reasons=[CONFIG_UNAVAILABLE, CONFIG_ATTRIBUTION_UNVERIFIED]. Those reasons are not in UNVERIFIED_REASONS (agent.py:20, 366-367), so neither ok=1 nor ok=0 telemetry/doa is sent, and bulk gets SOURCE_NOT_ELIGIBLE (agent.py:421). …

#### NEC-2 · HIGH · confirmed · data-correctness
**The 'raw DoA' from doa.xml is theta but the CSV raw is 360-theta; Edge and Ground show different numbers for the same bearing, and Ground flips between them**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/source.py:89`

- **Masalah:** Upstream writes the CSV bearing as 360 - theta_0 (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:759), but writes doa.xml <DOA> as theta_0 itself (DOA_str = f"{self.theta_0_list[0]}" at :792, passed to wr_xml at :1123). parse_doa_xml stores the XML value as raw_doa_deg without any convention handling, and everything downstream treats both values as the same 'raw' quantity. In the default commissioning state (authority_verified/angle_verified false), the Edge panel shows the doa.xml value under 'DOA RAW / UNVERIFIED' (app.js:290-291). The Edge sends Ground the CSV raw value as telemetry/doa ok=0 'a' with angle_reference RAW (agent.py:398-403). Ground's snapshot shows the CSV value whenever an ok=0 DoA arrived in the last 5 s, and otherwise falls back to the doa.xml value (ground.py:254-259). renderDiagnostic prints both as 'DoA raw: X°' and never names the source (ground.js:249). One physical bearing therefore reads 123° on the Edge and 237° on Ground. The Ground value jumps to 123° whenever the ok=0 path stops (e.g. DAQ recalibration after a retune) and back when it resumes. MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:50 calls the diagnostic value 'Sudut raw doa.xml' and IMPLEMENTATION.md:156-157 says DOA is kept raw, with no note that it uses the opposite convention from the CSV.
- **Dampak:** During commissioning, which is exactly when operators compare raw bearings to decide on angle_mode and angle_verified, the two panels disagree by 360-2θ degrees. The Ground reading also changes source silently, which can lead to a wrong orientation approval.
- **Bukti:** Reproduced with the simulator (run_scenarios.py E, authority/angle unverified, engine theta=123): Edge detection raw_doa_deg(CSV)=237.0; Edge panel diagnostic (doa.xml) raw_doa_deg=123.0 shown as 'DOA RAW / UNVERIFIED'; Ground diagnostic_doa source=DOA_value.html raw_doa_deg=237.0. After forcing a 12 s DAQ calibration: Ground diagnostic_doa source=doa.xml raw_doa_deg=123.0 stale=False, while the Edge panel still shows 123.0. Upstream lines checked in the scratchpad clone.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Normalize doa.xml to the CSV raw convention when parsing (raw = (360 - DOA) % 360), or carry an explicit 'convention' field and convert it before display. Show the source (DOA_value.html vs doa.xml) in the Ground diagnostic block, and do not merge the two sources into one slot without the conversion. Document the upstream convention difference.
- **Verifikasi:** Upstream writes the CSV bearing as 360-theta_0 (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:759) but doa.xml <DOA> as theta_0 (DOA_str at :792 passed to wr_xml; xml_doa.text=doa at :1123). parse_doa_xml (source.py:89) stores it as raw_doa_deg with no conversion. With the shipped defaults (authority_verified/angle_verified false), app.js:290-291 shows the doa.xml value as 'DOA RAW / UNVERIFIED'. Ground stores the ok=0 CSV 'a' under the same diagnostic slot (ground.py:188-192). Its snapshot prefers that value while it is under 5 s old and otherwise falls back to doa.xml (ground.py:254-259). renderDiagnostic (ground.js:249) prints 'DoA raw' without naming the source. The same bearing therefore reads theta …

#### NEC-4 · MEDIUM · confirmed · data-correctness
**Squelch patches are silently replaced by the engine (Auto squelch default) or truncated (Manual), yet the Edge reports persisted:true**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/control.py:186`

- **Masalah:** Upstream's default squelch mode is Auto (vfo_squelch_mode_0='Default' with vfo_default_squelch_mode='Auto'). In that mode mean_spectrum() overwrites vfo_squelch[i] every frame with the spectrum mean + 5 dB (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:261-273). save_configuration then writes that float back as vfo_squelch_0 (kraken_web_interface.py:347), and it does so on every ext_upd_flag write-back. In Manual mode the watcher applies int(vfo_squelch_0) (utils.py:359), so a fractional value is truncated and the truncated value is written back. The Edge ignores the squelch mode entirely: FIELD_MAP has no mode, helper.validate_changes accepts any float from -200 to 50 (helper.py:47), and the Ground field has step=any. A Ground squelch patch of -70 in Auto mode therefore ends with settings.json holding about -91.2 (an auto value). The verify loop never matches and returns PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED {persisted:true, revision:N}, but revision N's file content does not contain the requested value. The ACK, Ground text ('File settings tersimpan') and Edge toast all imply the value was stored. After Refresh, the Ground form shows the auto value with 14 decimals as if it were an editable setting. In Auto mode every patch of any field also bumps the revision a second time with a new auto value.
- **Dampak:** Operators believe they changed the squelch when the engine discarded or altered it. The settings view shows a fluctuating auto-squelch number as a configured value.

#### NEC-5 · MEDIUM · confirmed · data-correctness
**parse_csv rejects every DoA record for VFO settings that upstream writes routinely (float vfo_freq_0, output_vfo ALL, VFO Auto mode)**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/source.py:66`

- **Masalah:** The VFO-authority block calls integer(settings['vfo_freq_0']) and integer(settings['output_vfo'],0,15) (source.py:62-68). Upstream stores VFO frequencies as floats: the watcher does vfo_freq[i]=float(...) (utils.py:355), and when a center change in the KrakenSDR UI pushes VFOs out of band, update_daq_params resets them with vfo_freq[i]=f0*10**6 (callbacks/update_daq_params.py:28). For many MHz inputs that product is not an integer: e.g. 128.05 -> 128050000.00000001, 32.0125 -> ...; this hits 199 of the 1521 12.5 kHz airband channels (118-137 MHz) and 3135 grid channels overall. integer() raises INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE, so every record is rejected and the panel shows SOURCE_READ 'INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE', although safe_settings rounds the same field without complaint. The same block rejects the upstream output VFO option 'ALL' (output_vfo=-1, vfo_config_card.py:159) even with one active VFO. It also rejects every record in VFO Auto mode (vfo_mode='Auto'), where upstream writes the peak frequency into the record (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:518-521, 589) while settings keep the configured VFO (VFO_RECORD_AMBIGUOUS_OR_MISSING). None of these is documented as unsupported.
- **Dampak:** After a normal retune in the KrakenSDR web UI to an affected frequency (common in the 128-134 MHz airband), or with output VFO 'ALL', the node produces no DoA at all until someone rewrites the VFO frequency as an integer.
- **Bukti:** Parser repro with upstream-shaped settings and CSV (scratchpad/audit/sim/upstream_fmt.py): settings.json vfo_freq_0 = 128050000.00000001 (safe_settings gives 128050000) -> parse_csv rejected: INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE; output_vfo=-1 -> rejected: INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE; vfo_mode=Auto -> rejected: VFO_RECORD_AMBIGUOUS_OR_MISSING. Channel counts come from a Python enumeration of f*10**6 != int(f*10**6).
- **Saran perbaikan:** Parse vfo_freq_0 as a finite float and compare int(value), upstream's own int(), with the record frequency. Treat output_vfo -1 as equivalent to 0 when active_vfos == 1. Detect vfo_mode 'Auto' and report it as an explicit unsupported or unverified state instead of a generic parse error.
- **Verifikasi:** Upstream update_daq_params.py:31 sets vfo_freq = f0*10**6 (a float) when a center change in the Kraken UI pushes the VFO out of band. The watcher keeps it as a float (utils.py:355), while the CSV writes int(vfo_freq) (processor :589). I reproduced it: 128.05*10**6 = 128050000.00000001 (128.2 gives 128199999.99999999). integer() at source.py:68 raises INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE, so parse_csv rejects every record, while safe_settings rounds the same field without complaint. 199 of 1521 airband 12.5 kHz channels are affected. Upstream offers output VFO 'ALL' (-1) (vfo_config_card.py:159), and integer(-1,0,15) at source.py:63 also raises INTEGER_OUT_OF_RANGE in my repro. The vfo_mode Auto sub-point …

#### NEC-3 · LOW · confirmed · contract-mismatch
**With the real engine no UI settings change can ever reach APPLIED; every change holds the worker for 12 s and blanks LIVE DoA ~13 s and angular ~32 s**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/control.py:178`

- **Masalah:** The verify loop needs status.json daq_status.rf_center_frequency_hz/rf_center_freq to prove center_frequency_hz, and daq_status.gain_db to prove gain_db (control.py:176-181, source.py:111-112). Bandwidth and squelch have no proof path at all. Upstream save_processing_status (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:295-346) never writes rf_center_freq or any gain; the IQ header has rf_center_freq in Hz and if_gains in 0.1 dB per channel, but neither is exported. Both UIs always send center and VFO0 together (app.js:841, ground.js:351-352), and the Ground form otherwise only offers bandwidth, gain and squelch. As a result: (a) every settings change from either UI runs the loop until verify_seconds expires and ends PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED (amber); (b) the single worker stays busy for the full 12 s, so detection carries CONTROL_IN_PROGRESS for 12 s and Bulk angular then waits resume_stable_seconds (20 s) more; (c) config_proof becomes 'persisted_unverified' after every change. The APPLIED/FRESH_DAQ_RF_CENTER and FRESH_DAQ_GAIN outcomes described in CONTROL.md:86-88 and in the MQTT_TOPIC_SUMMARY.md:534 ACK example cannot happen with the upstream engine; README:369-370 only says 'some engines'. The engine's own acknowledgement, the ext_upd_flag:false write-back after it applies the file, is ignored.
- **Dampak:** Operators never see a verified retune, so a change the engine actually applied and one it ignored look the same. Every tweak, even one the engine discards (see NEC-4), costs ~13 s of LIVE DoA and ~32 s of 360° data over the link.
- **Bukti:** Simulator with upstream-shaped status.json (run_scenarios.py A, Edge panel PIN flow through /api/v2/commands): stage PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED proof {vfo0_frequency_hz:FRESH_DOA_FREQUENCY} reason NATIVE_RUNTIME_EVIDENCE_INCOMPLETE; worker busy until +12.1 s; detection.valid again +12.4 s; first telemetry/doa ok=1 +13.1 s; first telemetry/angular +32.3 s. Ground-originated gain-only, squelch-only and (separately) bandwidth-only runs (B scenarios): PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED with proof {}, worker busy +12.4 s, first angular +34.2 s. Only a VFO-only patch, which neither UI can send, reached APPLIED (+1.5 s, scenario C). parse_status on an upstream status object returns rf_center_frequency_hz=None, …
- **Saran perbaikan:** Use evidence the engine really produces: the ext_upd_flag true->false write-back with matching values, then a fresh healthy DAQ status and a record at the VFO frequency newer than the write-back. Report PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED as soon as all obtainable evidence is in, instead of waiting the full verify_seconds. Fix the doc examples, which show impossible APPLIED/FRESH_DAQ_RF_CENTER results. Consider a shorter bulk resume after a settings-only change.
- **Verifikasi:** The mechanism is real. Upstream save_processing_status (kraken_sdr_signal_processor.py:295-346) writes no rf_center_freq or gain, so parse_status (source.py:111-112) gets None for both. Bandwidth and squelch have no proof branch at all (control.py:176-181). Both UIs always send center and VFO together (app.js:841, ground.js:351-352). Any UI patch therefore loops until verify_seconds (12 s) with CONTROL_IN_PROGRESS masking detection (agent.py:243). The worker's finally block then adds resume_stable_seconds (20 s) before bulk resumes (control.py:149). However, the 'never APPLIED / PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED' result is documented on purpose: CONTROL.md:84-93 says bandwidth/squelch yield …

#### NEC-6 · LOW · confirmed · data-correctness
**The helper writes center_freq as Hz/1e6 and upstream truncates it, so ~1% of 12.5 kHz channels (e.g. 128.2 MHz airband) tune 1 Hz low and the Ground frequency field goes blank**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/helper.py:84`

- **Masalah:** patch_file stores center_freq = center_frequency_hz/1e6. Upstream's config_daq_rf calls set_center_freq(int(f0*10**6)) (kraken_web_interface.py:468), which truncates. For 1,568 of 139,361 channels on the 12.5 kHz grid (32-33, 64-67, 128-134, 256-268, 512-536 and 1024+ MHz), (hz/1e6)*1e6 falls just below hz, so the DAQ tunes hz-1. The write-back then stores center_freq=(hz-1)/1e6. The Edge then reports center_frequency_hz=hz-1 while vfo0_frequency_hz=hz. Effects: (a) the verify condition all(safe==changes) fails after the write-back, so even the VFO proof is lost (proof {}); (b) the revision changes again; (c) ground.js:285-286 sets settingsBase.frequency_hz to null whenever center!==vfo, so after Refresh the Ground frequency field is empty for that node. This is the engine-side conversion, not the known Ground MHz->Hz form conversion.
- **Dampak:** Retunes to affected channels can never show any proof. The RF center is 1 Hz off, which is harmless for RF, but the Ground can no longer display the node frequency.
- **Bukti:** Simulator G: Ground config.patch center=vfo=128200000 -> PERSISTED_UNVERIFIED proof {}; engine daq_center_hz=128199999; Edge safe {center:128199999, vfo:128200000}; Ground safe after Refresh is the same, giving ground.js settingsBase.frequency_hz = None. Enumeration: int((hz/1e6)*10**6)!=hz for 1568/139361 grid channels.
- **Saran perbaikan:** In the helper, choose the float so that int(x*10**6)==hz (e.g. if int(x*1e6)<hz use math.nextafter(x, math.inf)), and verify that before writing. Alternatively, compare center_frequency_hz with ±1 Hz tolerance in the Edge and Ground.
- **Verifikasi:** helper.py:84 writes center_freq = hz/1e6. Upstream config_daq_rf calls set_center_freq(int(f0*10**6)) (kraken_web_interface.py:468), and the receiver stores int(center_freq) (kraken_sdr_receiver.py:345). Verified: 128200000/1e6*1e6 = 128199999.99999999, which truncates to 128199999. This affects 1568 of 139361 channels on the 12.5 kHz grid. save_configuration then writes center 128.199999, so safe center 128199999 != vfo 128200000. The verify all(safe==changes) at control.py:175 fails, so even the VFO proof is lost, and ground.js:285-286 sets frequency_hz to null, leaving the field blank. This does not block other edits (validateSettings tolerates an empty field when the base is null). The …

#### NEC-7 · LOW · confirmed · data-correctness
**Config attribution starts at the file's write time, not when the engine applies it; records with the old gain/bandwidth/squelch can be attributed to the new revision**  
`rdf-node/src/rdf_node/source.py:146`

- **Masalah:** config_mtime_ms is set to settings.json's mtime when the Edge first sees a new safe digest (source.py:143-147). Attribution accepts any record with timestamp >= that mtime that was read after the digest change (source.py:232-233). Upstream applies a file only when its 0.5 s watcher reloads it (utils.py:245-417), so any writer that sets ext_upd_flag (the Edge helper, upstream's Node.js POST /settings and the Kraken Pro remote websocket, _nodejs/index.js:121-130 and 195-206) leaves a gap of up to ~0.5 s plus one CPI. In that gap, records computed with the old gain, bandwidth or squelch pass the check. The VFO filter (source.py:66-71) only catches frequency changes. If the Edge's settings poll lands inside the gap (~25% of patches; otherwise the engine write-back moves mtime past the apply), such records get config_attributed=True and the new revision. For Edge-originated patches only telemetry/diagnostic/angular (FLAG_CONFIG + new revision) and the Edge snapshot see this, because CONTROL_IN_PROGRESS masks LIVE. For upstream external-API writers in read-only deployments there is no mutation flag, so such a record is published as LIVE telemetry/doa or angular under the new revision.
- **Dampak:** Occasional old-config DoA or angular frames are labelled with the new configuration revision. This is mostly hidden for Edge-initiated changes but visible for changes made through upstream's own remote API.
- **Bukti:** Deterministic Source-level repro (scratchpad/audit/tmp/attrib): helper.patch_file bandwidth 12500->25000, Source.poll, then a record from the engine still on the old bandwidth -> config_attributed=True revision=2 (new) valid=True reasons=[]. Simulator I (manual squelch, 4 bandwidth patches) showed config_mtime equal to the engine write-back time in 4/4 runs (+270/+224/+190/+139 ms, matching the apply). The masking therefore depends on the poll falling after the write-back.
- **Saran perbaikan:** Do not attribute records to a revision whose file still has ext_upd_flag:true. Start attribution at the mtime of the engine's ext_upd_flag:false write-back, or at the first fresh status after it, rather than at the first mtime seen.
- **Verifikasi:** I reproduced it at the Source level. After a helper-style write of vfo_bw_0 12500->25000 with ext_upd_flag, a record whose timestamp is after the write (engine not yet applied) gives config_attributed=True, revision=2, valid=True. config_mtime_ms is the mtime of the first new digest seen (source.py:143-147), and attribution only checks timestamp >= that mtime (source.py:232-233). The upstream watcher applies changes only on its 0.5 s timer (utils.py:244-417). For Edge-originated patches CONTROL_IN_PROGRESS masks LIVE, so only diagnostics and the snapshot are mislabeled. With external writers in read-only mode, a few frames within about 0.5 s can carry the new revision label. Frequency …
